### 18.10 UNIQUENESS NOTE (step 18.2 a, web search 2026-11-15; NOT exhaustive, it does NOT guarantee that nothing like it exists)
Nearest existing bridges found (all facts as given by the sources named):
| Bridge | What it is | Source |
|--------|-----------|--------|
| San Francisco-Oakland Bay Bridge east span (USA, 2013) | self-anchored suspension, ONE vertical steel tower 160 m (four shafts), main span 385 m + back span 180 m, two steel orthotropic box girders, one cable looped round the west pier and anchored in the deck at the east pier, 78.7 m wide, bike path | Guinness World Records, Nader and Maroney (Structure magazine / SMIP04 paper), Wikipedia |
| Yeongjong Grand Bridge (Korea, 2000) | two-tower three-span self-anchored suspension, double deck (road + rail), main suspension section 550 m | Wikipedia, Structurae |
| Sorok Bridge (Korea) | mono-cable self-anchored suspension, 110 + 250 + 110 m, deck 15.7 m wide and 2.5 m deep, concrete pylon 87.4 m | Structurae |
| Konohana Bridge (Osaka, 1990) | self-anchored suspension with INCLINED HANGERS, described as the first of its kind (span not read) | Structurae / Structural Engineering International 1992 |
| Jiasong Highway Huangpu bridge (China) | two-pylon self-anchored suspension, double cable plane, composite steel box, 130 + 336 + 130 m, portal-frame pylons; the same paper proposes a PARTIAL ground anchor and UHPC deck | Tongji Prestress Technology 2023 / 2025 articles |
| Yunlongwan Bridge (China) | self-anchored suspension with an ultra-wide double-sided steel box | Structurae literature entry |
| Peace Bridge Derry (UK), Harbor Drive footbridge San Diego (USA) | self-anchored suspension FOOTBRIDGES with ONE INCLINED pylon (steel / concrete) | SteelConstruction.info, Aspire |
What the IB design must keep different (the claim is limited to the sources above): in everything found, the ROAD self-anchored suspension bridges have VERTICAL towers (one tower, or two portal-frame pylons); inclined pylons appear only on small single-pylon footbridges, and inclined hangers (Konohana) are a hanger idea, not a pylon idea. IB is: a PAIR of LEANING sail-shaped pylons on a 33.8 m wide 3 + 3 lane deck, one continuous box girder carrying the cable compression, cables anchored in the deck, over a shallow strait (0-7.4 m of water) with the deck about 10-11 m over the water. The shape and the unusual loading (very wide deck, shallow water, cyclone wind) are the points to design and to show in the tour. No "world-unique" promise is made: Structurae lists many self-anchored suspension bridges and this search did not read the full list. Ideas to avoid because they exist: single vertical tower (Bay Bridge), portal-frame twin pylons (Chinese urban bridges), inclined hangers as the only novelty (Konohana).
Sources used for 18.2 numbers: BNBC 2020 wind table (Bagerhat 77.5 m/s) and seismic zone table (Bagerhat Z 0.12), seen as a Scribd copy of the gazette pages, NOT checked against the official text; cyclone history from icddr,b (cch.icddrb.org) and the BMD list; EN 1991-1-5 Type 1 values (dTM 18 / 13 K, omegaN 0.35, omegaM 0.75) from Dlubal, Structville and an Acta Polytechnica paper; ICCESD 2026 (KUET) paper for "no Bangladesh bridge code, AASHTO or Eurocode used".

### 21.0 What the user said (data, do not change without asking)

### 21.7 DECISIONS OF THE USER, 2026-11-17 (data, do not change without asking)
1. His centrelines are roughly FIXED ("amar line motamoti fixed, besi change nai, ami onek vebe chinta korci"). Only small shifts are allowed, and only where an analysis finds a fault ("halka adik odik hobe jodi analysis kore fail pawa jai"). Example he gave: where a line runs into a hill, go along the hillside round it, so NO tunnel is needed ("paharer ga ghese road jabe tahole tunnel dorkar nai"). So the 48 TUNNEL-CANDIDATES of 21.6 are not tunnels: step 21.3 tries a small shift along the hillside first (R12); a tunnel stays the very last choice and is never taken silently.
2. BUILDINGS and ENGINEERING SITES are relocated LATER ("building / engineering site pore relocate hobe"). The 68 building hits and the engineering area on R2-74 are NOT a reason to move a line now; they go into a relocation list.
3. R6 may be reshaped a little ("r6 hakla reshape lagte pare"); which built R6 (R6-7 named earlier) is still to be shown by the analysis in 21.3 (R6-9 runs along the built R6-7 at 3 degrees: first case).
4. The 1600 m rule: the user said: keep it if it is a fixed ENGINEERING rule, change it if it is not. AI answer (from its own knowledge, NOT looked up on the web): the 1.6 km (about 1 mile) urban / 2 km rural spacing is a standard design guideline for INTERCHANGES on a motorway (section 3.8), and section 3.8 also says an at-grade junction is collector to local only and never a local street straight onto a motorway or divided arterial. It is a guideline, not a law, but it is the normal engineering practice, so it is KEPT. CONSEQUENCE (21.1 R5 list): 46 nodes of his lines touch an 8-lane road (M8 or a J1 arm), all closer than 1600 m to a tap or each other, and many of them are R2 / C / L ends. Step 21.3 must find, for every such node, the smallest fix: the small road ends at the nearest R4 / R6 road (a short shift) and only R6 / R4 reach the 8-lane road at a tap; the list of kept taps and the shifts are printed and the user judges them.
5. R4-26 is NO problem: keep it. It ends on J1-N as a T junction (21.1: J1-N x R4-26 at (1333, -2189), 2281 m from the J1 plaza). The user wants that T a little FARTHER from J1 if it can be moved, never closer.

(3) HILL shift: dynamic programme on 10 m stations along the normal, offsets +-25 m (tier 1) then +-60 m (tier 2), cost = grade over the class limit (squared) + water + rail within 25 m + other road within 18 m + bends; junction points and ends are pinned; a result is kept only if over-limit metres drop by 15 % or more, no water or rail crossing is added and no NEW crossing appears (R1). (4) RADIUS: local smoothing, cap 35 m, until the radius is at least the class minimum (design speed R6 V80 229 m, R4 V60 113 m, R2 and C V40 41 m, L V30 20 m = my choice). Curvature is made by smoothing the polyline: the real straight - spiral - arc - spiral - straight pieces are fitted at build time (21.6).
RESULT: 10 join proposals checked: 3 applied (R4-16 -> R4-17 43 m, R4-26 end -> J1-N 11 m, L-15 -> R2-13 38 m), 7 not (R6-9 -> R6-7 shallow 3 deg = the reshape case, R4-15 would cross a road, R4-26 start -> R2-70 smaller road, and 4 joins of R2-13, C-8, C-9, L-21 to an 8-lane road refused by R5). ### 21.12 STEP 21.4c NODE LEVELS + PARABOLIC VERTICAL CURVES (2026-11-17, headless scripts/centre-vprofile.ts RUN; nothing in src or the key-3 data changed)
USER ORDER: new script scripts/centre-vprofile.ts, output only in docs/, nothing in src changes; first the WATER CHECK, then fix the level at every node, then the parabola vertical curves.
Files: NEW scripts/centre-vprofile.ts (run: npx tsx scripts/centre-vprofile.ts docs; env NONODES=1 = curves only, for comparison), docs/STEP_21_4C_OUTPUT.txt (all lists, one row per line), docs/centre_vprofile.json (node table with the agreed level + per line PVI table [s, y] + curve table s / A / L / K / crest or sag). Reads docs/centre_profile.json (21.4 DP) and docs/centre_fixed.json.
METHOD (my numbers, the user may change them, all constants are at the top of the script):
0. WATER: sc.water > sc.h on the 10 m stations of the 154 lines: 37 wet stations (370 m) on 10 lines (R6-10, R6-11, R4-20, R4-23, R4-24, R4-29, R2-19, R2-55, R2-68, R2-70), deck must be >= water + 1.5 m. The 21.4 profile had 1 wet station under that level; after the curves ONE line is still low (R4-24, 1 station): NOT fixed, goes to 21.8 (bridge design).
1. NODES: recomputed from the fixed lines (an end within 12 m of another line, or a crossing; merged within 15 m): 136 nodes between the profiled lines. 40 more line ends lie on a built / 8-lane road (their level is NOT matched here, the level of M8 / J1 arms / built R6 is not read yet).
2. NODE LEVEL: relaxation, 6 rounds (every line solved with soft pins, node level = mean of its legs weighted by class rank squared: R6 25, R4 16, R2 9, C 4, L 1, so the bigger road moves less), then one final level DP with HARD pins on every line (grade <= 92 % of the class limit so that the fitted tangents stay under the limit; lowered to 85 / 78 / 70 % for a line that still goes over). A first try 'the first line sets the level, the others follow' gave 40 to 50 m cuts on short steep lines (R6-12, R4-9) and was dropped.
3. CURVES: PVIs by Douglas-Peucker (smallest tolerance 0.1 .. 1.5 m whose curves do not overlap; node stations are forced PVIs), curve K from the SSD of the design speed (R6 V80, R4 V60, R2 / C V40, L V30 = 21.3 choice), L = max(K |A|, 0.6 V), grade change < 0.5 % = no curve. The exact level at every node is then set on the parabola (minimum-norm change of the PVI levels, curve lengths held).
RESULT: 154 lines, 606 interior PVIs, 551 parabolic curves (263 crest, 288 sag), 55 grade breaks under 0.5 % without a curve, 60 PVIs dropped for overlap, 1 node PVI made a kink. Grade over the class limit: 0 lines. NODE LEVELS: before (21.4) 97 of 136 nodes differed by more than 0.03 m (max 31.9 m); now 132 of 136 agree within 0.03 m (curve level at every leg). 4 nodes stay OPEN (the line cannot take the node level inside its grade limit between two pinned nodes, the line keeps its own level there; real conflicts, need a structure, a moved node or a bigger cut / fill): N004 (-237, -2195) R6-12 47.9 m | R4-9 82.9 m (35 m: R6-12 is only 268 m long and its ground climbs 29 -> 78 m, 18 % natural grade), N030 (-1760, -524) R4-29 28.3 | R6-15 37.2 (8.9 m), N075 (4498, 2844) R2-54 55.8 | R4-21 44.1 (11.7 m), N108 (1428, -2568) L-10 30.6 | R4-10 27.4 (3.2 m).
COST (tunnel-candidate = cut over 12 m / viaduct = fill over 7 m, metres): the free 21.4 profile 1640 / 4460; with curves only (NONODES=1) 2240 / 5740; with node levels + curves 4010 / 8650 (R6 200 / 530 -> 480 / 1370, R4 460 / 1280 -> 1480 / 2030, R2 960 / 2620 -> 2030 / 5170, C 20 / 30 -> 20 / 80, L 0 / 0). So most of the rise comes from holding ONE level at the nodes. The PVI fit tolerance had to go to 1.5 m on 60 of the 154 lines (the DP level is noisy), so the curves follow the DP level within 1.5 m only between the nodes. A joint PVI optimiser with the cut / fill cost inside (not a fit of the DP result) would bring the cost down: that is the proposal for the next sub-step, NOT done.
NOT DONE: level of the 40 line ends on built / 8-lane roads, rail crossings, building clearance, drainage (min grade 0.3 %), junction cross-grade, deck depth of bridges, R4-24 wet station, npm run build and the game NOT run (the script is read only, no src file touched; node_modules was installed with npm ci in this chat, npx tsc --noEmit of the whole project ran with no output).
RUN ORDER now: ... centre-profile.ts again (21.4b order above), then npx tsx scripts/centre-vprofile.ts docs.

PVI OPTIMISER (pvCost / optimisePvi): PVI levels and stations are moved (coordinate search, steps 4 .. 0.1 m) to minimise the TRUE cost on the final curves (cut / fill / tunnel / viaduct / wet freeboard), hard: class grade limit, node levels, curve overlap; accepted only if cheaper than the DP fit. 145 of 154 lines improved.

3. RAIL CROSSINGS (9 on 7 lines): rail design level (e.design) taken as given; road over rail needs road >= rail + 7.4 m, road under rail needs road <= rail - 7.4 m inside +-40 m (RAIL_DESIGN.overRoadRise); the side is chosen from the DP level and flipped if the grade limit cannot take it. RESULT 8 of 9 clear (R6-9 over, R2-28 x2 under, R2-33 over, R2-74 over, R4-20 x2 under, R6-15 under). R6-11 x ring-northeast (s 1263) stays NOT clear (road 1.96 m over the rail): its end is pinned to the M8 level 28.10 m only 180 m behind the crossing, 4 % grade cannot reach 7.4 m: USER DECISION (move the line, structure in 21.8, or change the rail level).
4. JUNCTION APPROACH GRADE (my number, no rule in the handoff): every non-top-class leg of a node and every line end on a built road keeps |grade| <= 3 % inside +-30 m of the node (soft cost in the optimiser, then checked): 156 legs, 120 ok, 36 over 3 % (all listed in 3c; the class limit still holds, they sit on steep ground).
5. DRAINAGE: a tangent of 60 m or more needs |grade| >= 0.3 % (cost in the optimiser, a flat tangent between two equal pinned levels gets a crest PVI in the middle). 2 flat tangents are left: R4-6 s 65 (826 m, 0.04 %) and R4-10 s 770 (120 m, 0.29 %): drain / cross-fall design in 21.5.
6. WET STATIONS: freeboard (water + 1.5 m) is held BEFORE the rail clearance (a rail viaduct can be raised, a road bridge cannot be lowered). 2 stations still low: R4-24 s 971 by 0.27 m (x 3075, z -3079) and R4-20 s 1502 by 0.31 m (x -85, z 1647): bridge deck in 21.8.

### 21.18 STEP 21.6c EARTHWORK / FORMATION (2026-11-17, headless scripts/centre-earthwork.ts RUN; nothing in src changed; tsc / npm run build / game NOT RUN: no node_modules)
Files: NEW scripts/centre-earthwork.ts (run: npx tsx scripts/centre-earthwork.ts docs; 3 s; needs docs/centre_profile.json, centre_vprofile.json, centre_meshsec.json), docs/centre_earthwork.json (per class / section / line, structure list, ROW overruns), docs/STEP_21_6C_OUTPUT.txt.
METHOD (differs from 21.5): the REAL section of every stretch (OPEN / ZONE, widths blended inside the 30 m taper of 21.6b); the ground is sampled ACROSS the road every 0.5 m on both sides (21.5 used the centre ground all across); subgrade = finished surface - pavement thickness inside the paved edge; cut slope 1:2 starts at the ditch bottom (OPEN) or formation edge (ZONE), fill slope 1:3 starts at the shoulder edge; both are followed to the catch point on the real ground (search 60 m beyond the formation). NOT in the numbers: topsoil strip, shrinkage, rock, ditch toe in fills, junction widening. Constants at the top of the script.
RESULT (154 lines, 114.1 km): cut 2.47 Mm3, fill 1.20 Mm3 (net 1.27 Mm3 surplus). By class (cut / fill Mm3): R6 0.39 / 0.22, R4 1.08 / 0.54, R2 0.88 / 0.35, C 0.06 / 0.04, L 0.06 / 0.05. NOT comparable with the 21.5 totals (4.18 / 1.78): 21.5 counted every station with a centre-only section, this step takes the side-hill stations out as structures (below).
STRUCTURES FOR 21.8 (metres, merged stretches with line and s in the output): tunnel-candidate (centre cut > 12 m) 3.4 km in 48 stretches (largest R4-9 s 2176-2614, 438 m, max cut 36.6 m); viaduct (centre fill > 7 m) 7.9 km in 76 stretches (largest R4-24 s 0-515, 515 m, max fill 29.3 m); bridge (wet) 0.37 km in 10 stretches; NEW: SIDE-HILL 18.9 km in 232 stretches = the cut / fill slope does not catch the ground within 60 m (9.2 km) or its face is higher than 12 m cut / 7 m fill (9.7 km); this is a retaining wall / half viaduct / moved line question (R12: first a small shift along the hillside, tunnel last) and it is MY criterion, the user may change the limits. Biggest: R2-2 s 325-1686 (1361 m), R2-6 s 465-1638 (1172 m), R4-24 s 1727-2587 (860 m).
ROW: the catch point lies outside the ROW on 55 km of the volume stations, more than 5 m outside on 20.2 km (109 lines; R4 6.7 km, R2 10.9 km, C 0.7, L 1.5, R6 0.1). Cause: the ZONE sections keep only 0.2 - 0.5 m of ROW beyond the formation, so any cut / fill of more than about 0.5 m leaves the strip. Decision for the user (not taken): wider ROW in cuts / fills, or steeper slope with a wall, or accept (land take).

RULE: e = eNeeded rounded UP to 0.5 %, at least 2 % (adverse crown removed), at most 8 % (CRITERIA.emax). R under the class minimum: e stays 8 % and the bend gets an advisory speed (largest V with Rmin(V) <= R).

### 21.22 STEP 21.6e-3 RUNOFF / RUNOUT vs SPIRAL + ROTATION AXIS (2026-11-17, headless scripts/centre-runoff.ts RUN; nothing in src changed; tsc / npm run build / game NOT RUN: no node_modules)
Files: NEW scripts/centre-runoff.ts (run: node --experimental-strip-types scripts/centre-runoff.ts docs, or npx tsx; reads docs/centre_super.json and docs/centre_sections.json), docs/centre_runoff.json (per bend with e > 0: axis, Lr, Lt, Ls, Ls_new, in-spiral flag), docs/STEP_21_6E3_OUTPUT.txt.
METHOD (the ring method of scripts/ring-superelevation.ts, my choices, the user may change them): w = lane width, n = lanes per direction, bw = (1 + 0.5 (n - 1)) / n, relative gradient 0.44 % (the ring PROJECT ASSUMPTION, used for every class: safe side for the slower classes); runoff Lr = w n bw e / 0.0044 (0 -> +e), runout Lt = w n bw 2 % / 0.0044 (-2 % -> 0), both rounded UP to 5 m; shoulders not in the width. ROTATION AXIS: divided classes (M8 / arms 4 lanes x 3.65 m, R6 3 x 3.5, R4 2 x 3.5; median 6 / 4 / 4 m) rotate about the median edge, the outer carriageway governs; undivided (R2 1 x 3.5, C 1 x 3.3, L 1 x 2.75) about the centreline. Handoff 3.2 rule: Ls not shorter than the runoff, so Ls_new = max(Ls, Lr rounded up to 10 m). The runout Lt stays on the tangent before the spiral unless Lr + Lt <= Ls_new (then it can lie inside the spiral, tangent runout 0, as the ring option inspiral).
LENGTHS (Lt / Lr at e 2 % / Lr at e 8 %): M8 45 / 45 / 170 m, R6 35 / 35 / 130, R4 25 / 25 / 100, R2 20 / 20 / 65, C 15 / 15 / 60, L 15 / 15 / 50.
RESULT (160 bends with e > 0): Ls already long enough 132, Ls raised 28 (R6 16, R4 12; biggest raise 40 m: R6-7 #1 and R6-8 #1 90 -> 130 m at e 8 %); Lr + Lt fits inside Ls_new for 66 bends (M8 1, R6 1, R4 51, R2 13), the rest keep a tangent runout; longest Ls_new R4-9 / R4-27 junction corners 350-360 m (these are R 22 m corners, see 21.21 MAX list, not real spirals); J1-W #1 Ls 300 m (R 123 m, V100, special case of 21.20).
CHECKS: ring worked value reproduced (M8 e 2 %: Lr 45 m, Lt 45 m, same as docs/ring-superelevation_nc018_output.txt), every bend has a row, relative gradient at most 0.44 %, Ls_new not shorter than Ls or Lr and a multiple of 10 m, axis by class, lengths positive: 6 PASS 0 FAIL. They test the table and the method, not the design.
NOT DONE: free straight between two bends (6 V same direction / 2 V reverse, tangent runouts inside it), bend length vs 2 Ls_new, junction nodes and R < Rmin corners (21.7), the real fit.
LIMIT: R, Ls and positions are the measured ones of 21.6e-1; 0.44 % for every class is to be confirmed.
NEXT: 21.6 e-4 FREE STRAIGHT BETWEEN BENDS + TANGENT RUNOUT + BEND LENGTH vs 2 Ls_new (the user says OK).

NEW src/terrain/ResidentialFlat.ts: `h = nat - w * flatShift(nat - level)`. LEVEL (m): nw-town 19.2, south-suburb 33.2, north-homes 20.3, east-suburb 30.2, east-homes 31.9, sw-homes 14.8 = median natural height of the flat / gentle (slope < 8 %) cells of the zone. CAP: cut / fill at most 8 m (flat exactly where |nat - level| <= 6 m, eases to 8 m, a hill inside the zone stays a hill: no mountain is cut away). BLEND 200 m outside the zone border (power-diagram cell of WorldZones.ts, convex, exact distance; engineering areas and facilities are keep-outs and keep their own pads). Water: the flat ground fades to natural over 90 m towards rivers / lakes (BANK_FADE; the river edge is exact up to about 99 m, so do not raise it above 90 without changing RiverNetwork.edgeDistance) and above sea level, so banks are never touched and nothing floods. Analytic, ~6-10 half-planes per zone, early exit outside the zone box, no table, no loop over the map (PERFORMANCE_RULES.md).
CHANGED: src/terrain/ResidentialFlat.ts (new), src/terrain/TerrainGenerator.ts (field `residentialFlat`, one call in sample() AFTER sampleGround and BEFORE roads.apply; sampleGround / sampleNatural are NOT changed, so roads, rail, rivers were planned on natural ground exactly as before), src/terrain/Lakes.ts + RiverNetwork.ts (new query-only method edgeDistance(), no height or water changed), scripts/residential-flat-scan.ts (flag --flat scans the flattened ground, default output = Step 1 json, byte-identical), scripts/residential-flat-check.ts (new, before / after), this file. ### 22.4 TASK 5: BLEND JUMP CHECK (check rewritten + two src tuning changes; headless RUN, game LOOK NOT RUN)
CHANGED: scripts/residential-flat-check.ts section 2 (+ PLOT_BLEND import), src/terrain/ResidentialFlat.ts (linearFade() for the water fade; plot pad constants), src/buildings/Placements.ts (house-brick-2 spot), this file. Zone blend (200 m) and cap (8 m) are NOT changed, so the borders between zones are not touched (matters for task 3).
OLD CHECK WAS WRONG: it counted a step as a "jump" when the natural step was <= 0.5 m and the flattened step > 0.5 m: a tiny shift could cross the line. NEW: for every 5 m step in the blend (outside the zone, not wet) it takes (flattened step) - (natural step) = how much steeper the flattening made it; a step that got flatter never counts; FAIL when that is > 0.5 m per 5 m. Also printed: how many steps are steeper by > 0.3 m (info) and the worst value.
WHY THE OLD NUMBERS WERE HIGH: the water fade (smoothstep over 90 m, steepest slope 1.5 / 90) with a shift of 8 m adds up to 0.67 m / 5 m, and the plot pad (blend 12 m) up to 2.4 m / 5 m. FIX: the water fade of the zones and of the pads is now LINEAR (steepest slope 1 / 90: 0.44 m / 5 m at 8 m shift; the edge of the water is still exact, the fade still starts at 0 at the bank); the plot pad is wider (50 m) and shallower (cap 2 m).
RESULT (RUN, residential-flat-check): section 2 PASS in nw-town (worst +0.46 m / 5 m), south-suburb (+0.30), north-homes (+0.49), east-suburb (+0.42), east-homes (+0.47); FAIL in sw-homes: 67 of 165898 steps (0.04 %), worst +0.61 m / 5 m near (-690, 660), the pad of the clinic at (-720, 640) (a 15 % hillside, range 3.75 m under the plot) on top of the zone cut; the zone alone has 15 steps over 0.5 (worst 0.53). The clinic was NOT moved (a clinic 220 m away is a design change); plot range under the clinic now 0.55 m (limit 0.6). Water / banks PASS x6, roads stay on the ground PASS x6, plots section 6 PASS (13), street cross slope PASS x5 + east-homes FAIL (4 points, needs RoadNetwork.apply, not changed). Total residential-flat-check: 2 FAIL.
Natural ground itself has steps > 0.5 m / 5 m on most cells; that is why only the added part is judged. Blend 200 m + cap 8 m were enough, no increase needed.

### 22.5 TASK 3: FOUR MORE ZONES FLATTENED: se-dense, west-homes, west-suburb, sw-newtown (headless checks RUN, game LOOK by the user NOT RUN)
USER DECISION (2026-11-18, final): road, rail, EVLab, city mall, J1 and the water lines (rivers / lakes) are FIXED; everything else may be relocated. Engineer decisions below are mine.
NOT FLATTENED ON PURPOSE: north-labour (80 % of the zone is more than 8 m from any level, median ground 68 m, only 113 gentle cells: a level with an 8 m cap would do nothing there; roads fixed, no plots, so nothing to do) and the 4 hill villages (76-95 % steep; flattening would remove the hills). Say so if you want them anyway.
CHANGED:
- src/terrain/ResidentialFlat.ts: (1) FLAT_ZONES + 4 rows: se-dense 30.3 m, west-homes 29.0 m, west-suburb 16.4 m, sw-newtown 19.3 m, blend 320 m (old 6 zones stay 200 m); level = median natural height of the gentle (slope < 8 %) dry cells that the 6 old zones do NOT already shift (a neighbour's blend would pull the level). Zone cut / fill gorosh (m): se-dense 5.5, west-homes 6.2, west-suburb 4.9, sw-newtown 5.8, max 8 for all; share of the zone more than 8 m from the level: 45 / 57 / 48 / 50 %. (2) FlatZoneDef.seaFade (new, optional): sea fade width above sea level, default 3 m; sw-newtown uses 30 m because its land reaches the beach and a 3-10 m fade made a 4-5 m cliff at the shore (+3.4 m / 5 m, then +1.1 m / 5 m). (3) apply(): zones no longer stack. Before, zone k shifted the ground that zone k-1 had already shifted, so where two zone blends overlap the cut / fill could reach 16 m (south-suburb, sw-homes, east-homes, all new zones did, check 1 FAIL). Now every zone pulls the NATURAL ground; if the zone weights add up to more than 1 the shifts are weight-averaged (cut / fill stays <= 8 m, the ground eases from one level to the other); the water fade multiplies the final shift (NOT the weights, a first version did and gave +0.7 m / 5 m near the central lake). With a weight sum <= 1 the result is the same as before. (4) PLOT_BLEND 50 -> 80 m (clinic pad step 0.61 -> below 0.5).

Full top 20 in docs/CUTFILL_MEASURE.txt section B.
CAUSE COUNT (places >= 0.5 m): along fill -> fill 554; cross cutwall -> free 380; cross plat -> free 374; cross plat -> cut 363; along cutwall -> cutwall 292; along two roads 283; cross plat -> fill 275; cross cut -> cut 271; cross fill -> fill 248.
CAUSE GUESS (code reading of RoadNetwork.apply(), NOT yet proven, step 2 tests it): (a) the daylight cut / fill takes ONLY the nearest segment (`if (d < dd)`), no blend: where the nearest segment (or the nearest road) switches, the target height switches too, so the ground jumps along the road (segment switch, two roads, T-junction mouths). (b) the clamp band lo / hi (`h < lo` / `h > hi`) is a hard min / max: where the natural ground crosses the band edge the slope changes sharply (plat -> free, cutwall -> free ledges). (c) cutwall: slope cut steeper than 1:1 is the road's designed cut slope (sca / scb), so a wall is a design value, not a blend error.
NOT RUN: npm install (no network; three / typescript folders are empty), tsc --noEmit (stops at missing vite/client), npm run build, game / browser LOOK. No other check was run in this step because no code changed.
STEP 2 (cause test, measure + script prototype only, NO src change; headless RUN):
CHANGED: scripts/cutfill-measure.ts (new env PROTO=1 = prototype of a blended daylight rule inside the script copy of apply(); new line "WHO MAKES IT"; new section E), docs/CUTFILL_MEASURE.txt (rerun, same numbers), docs/CUTFILL_MEASURE_PROTO.txt (prototype output), this section.
TEST 1 (guess (a) rejected as the MAIN cause): prototype = at a point take ALL daylight pieces in reach, weight 1 / run^2 (faded out over the last 10 m of reach), platform piece wins at run 0. Result: places >= 0.5 m 3674 -> 3682, >= 1 m 1739 -> 1895 (WORSE), and 168 sample points ON the road (offset <= halfWidth) changed. REJECTED, not applied. Side finding (real, small): inside a wide platform (M8, core 22.7 m) a nearer narrow side road wins the shoulder strip (81 points 13-22 m off the M8 centreline take the side road's slope, e.g. M8 i263 / i449 / i668): a separate small bug, candidate for a later step.
TEST 2 (who makes the places >= 0.5 m): ONE road reaches the point: 3211 of 3674 (87 %; >= 1 m: 1416 of 1739); earthwork of SEVERAL roads / a pad: 463 (13 %; >= 1 m: 323). So T-junction mouths are only a small part; the main cause sits inside the single road's own face. Single-road causes: along fill -> fill 508, cross cutwall -> free 358, cross plat -> free 355, cross plat -> cut 337, along cutwall -> cutwall 277, cross plat -> fill 264, cross cut -> cut 258, cross fill -> fill 240.
CAUSE NOW (code reading + numbers): the daylight face is a hard clamp, `h < lo ? lo : h > hi ? hi : h` (RoadNetwork.apply()): the 1:2 cut / 1:3 fill (steeper up to 1:0.25 on rock cuts, DAY_CUT_MAX 4) starts with a corner at the platform edge and ends with a corner where it meets the natural ground (the toe / the top of the cut). Those corners are the "ledges" (plat -> cut / plat -> fill / cut -> free / fill -> free). Also `reach` is built on the NATURAL ground and ends the face with a hard `d >= core + e` cut. Walls (cut slope steeper than 1:1) are the designed rock cut (DAY_CUT_MAX 4), they are not a blend error.
NEXT (step 3, ONE small fix, needs src/roads/RoadNetwork.ts apply()): round the two corners (platform edge fades in over the first metres so the road and its platform stay at 0.00 m; toe and top of cut eased with a smooth min / max of a few metres), only in the daylight branch; then before / after run of this script and the check list. Walls stay as designed unless you say they must be flatter (that widens the cut footprint).

DAYLIGHT STEP 3 DONE 2026-11-18 (only src/roads/RoadNetwork.ts changed): new constants DAY_SOFT 1.0 m and DAY_EASE_RUN 2 m; in apply() daylight branch a soft clamp rounds the corner where natural ground meets the cut line / fill line (softness 0 at the platform edge, so road and platform stay 0.00 m; it only cuts / fills a little MORE, never steeper). ring-cut-check BEFORE = 4 FAIL, AFTER = same 4 FAIL, same numbers (platform worst 9.727 m bad 69; envelope worst 8.64 m bad 58; no wall worst excess 1.89 bad 3 at 10198R 16077R 16117R; last offset worst 9.0 m walls 16); rock-cut face samples 823 -> 818; nothing new failed. These 4 FAIL are OLD (the same before this step). USER DECISION 2026-11-18: steep rock-cut walls must not stay; if more cut is needed, cut the terrain more (do not keep the wall). NEXT (step 4, one small change): make the cut face gentler where it is steeper than 1:1 (lower DAY_CUT_MAX in RoadNetwork.ts, wider cut) and rerun ring-cut-check; check where the 69 platform FAIL points come from (probably another road near the ring, e.g. pt 445 st 3559).

RESULT (RUN): both typical sheets 40 PASS 0 FAIL. 8-lane: dead 13.2 kN/m, bottom chord 216 MPa (limit 307), first frequency 5.3 Hz (>= 3 Hz walking limit), live deflection 10.6 mm (limit L/500 = 49 mm), wind 703 kN, wind moment per pier 1782 kNm, pile force 34 .. 508 kN (no uplift), dead weight of the 2 spans about 67 t. 6-lane: bottom chord 193 MPa, 7.9 Hz, 5.3 mm, wind 530 kN, pile force 60 .. 421 kN, about 46 t. Stairs for a foot at the road level: 43 risers of 149 mm in 4 flights (11+11+11+10), headroom under an upper flight 2.97 m (needs 2.2). FEET WINDOW (my choice): the ground at a stair foot must be -3 .. +3 m from the road surface (below -3 m the tower has more than 6 flights, above +3 m the pier stands in a road cut). ### 24.2 WHICH JUNCTION GETS AN INTERCHANGE (MY screening rules; the user may change them)
IR1 Any R4 / R6 end that touches an 8-lane road (M8 ring or a J1 arm) = INTERCHANGE, never at grade (user 2026-11-15 override of 3.9 + R5: only at a tap, through that node's interchange). That is tier B.
IR2 Ring x J1-S x island link = 4-leg grade-separated interchange (D17-1).
IR3 The ground forces it: N030 (R4-29 x R6-15, at grade 230 m at 6 %) and N074 (R2-54 x R4-21, at grade 99 m at 8 %) = grade-separated (already in 21.47 / 21.49).
IR4 R6 x R4 signalised T (R6 carries the heavy vehicles, 80 km/h): CONDITIONAL interchange candidates N002, N004, N008, N009, N024. They stay signalised T until the demand step says the signal fails (degree of saturation > 0.9).
IR5 Everything else stays AT GRADE (T, signalised T, roundabout): R4 x R4 (7 nodes: N011, N013, N016, N018, N019, N021, N028) and all 104 R2 / C / L nodes (rule X1: no + at grade, 4 arms = roundabout).
RESULT: 20 DEFINITE grade-separated sites (RT-S 1 + 17 taps + N030 + N074), 5 CONDITIONAL (N002, N004, N008, N009, N024), 4 big GROUND junctions (J1 built, ring N / E / W by the ring T handoff), 111 at grade (7 + 104). 20 + 5 + 4 + 111 = 140.

### 24.3 FACTS FROM THE DATA THAT DECIDE THE DESIGN (RUN)
1. LEVELS: for 21 of the 22 taps the minor road end is pinned to the 8-lane level (gap 0.0 - 0.15 m, 21.39). A grade separation needs a gap: the minor road must go OVER (M8 and the J1 arms are FIXED) by clearance + deck, about 7.8 m (21.60 found R6-11 over M8 at 35.90 m on M8 28.10 m). So every tap interchange re-runs the vertical profile of its minor road (R4 limit 6 %, R6 4 % / aim 3 %, R11) over about 150 - 260 m. T21 is the opposite case: R4-24 starts at 70.5 m on M8 34.54 m (gap 35.9 m, 21.7 'RAMP', 599 m ramp length): the minor road comes DOWN to M8.
2. MERGE GROUPS (same 8-lane road, gap < 600 m): T05 + T06 + T07 on J1-N (chain, 485 + 257 m, 743 m total), T11 + T12 on M8 (269 m), T16 + T18 on M8 (493 m), T19 + T20 on M8 (263 m). A trumpet needs about 2 x (taper 91 + decel 100) = 380 m (21.47 estimate) and a weave of 150 m minimum / 300 m desirable (3.8), so TWO separate trumpets do not fit in 257 - 269 m. PROPOSAL: one interchange per group with a collector-distributor (C-D) road, or one interchange with two minor legs joined away from the main road. 22 taps become 17 sites.
3. SKEW TAPS (angle to the 8-lane road < 70 deg): T10 (R6-15, 61, J1-W), T11 (R6-15, 61, M8), T18 (R4-14, 64), T19 (R4-23, 57). A ramp interchange wants about 90 deg: realign the last 150 - 250 m of the minor line to 90 deg first (as 21.49 did for N035 / N067 / N070), or choose a Y-type ramp for those.
4. SPACING on the ring (stations, m): RT-S 1205, T14 2090, T13 3575, RT-W 4265, T12 5344, T11 5614, T15 7589, T17 9869, T19 11169, T20 11429, RT-N 12094, T21 12734, T22 13428, RT-E 16813, T18 18733, T16 19228. Smallest gaps: 257 / 263 / 269 m (merge groups), RT-S to T14 885 m, RT-N to T20 665 m, RT-N to T21 640 m, RT-W to T13 690 m. The three limits 257 / 416 / 394 m of 21.43 are the AI decision (never confirmed in words).
5. NEIGHBOUR NODES: N004 (R6-12 x R4-9, signalised T) is 241 m from T04 (R4-9 on J1-N); N028 (R4-28 x R4-27, 50.8 deg, NO pocket, 21.51) is 233 m from T17 (R4-27 on M8). Design each pair together.
6. DEMAND: there is NO AADT for the new R6 / R4 / R2 lines (docs/a3_demand_x*.json is the old chord plan). IR4 needs a demand step (3.13) on the fitted network before it can be decided. Until then the ramp / lane sizes are from the class (R4 = 2 + 2, R6 = 3 + 3).
7. EXISTING WORK to reuse: T22 trumpet attempt (21.60, docs/b7_3a_t22*, tools/b7-3a-t22.py: m1 / m2 / m3 PASS, m4 not solved), R6-8 half interchange + slips (src/roads/r6/R6SlipData.ts), ring T handoff (HANDOFF_RING_T_JUNCTIONS.md), J1 pattern (src/roads/junction/OpenJ1*.ts, GroundJ1Terrain.ts), src/interchange/* (Geometry2D, Validate, Standards, Patterns), 21.48 T / roundabout numbers (docs/centre_junction_geom.json, 83 T + 37 roundabouts).

### 24.4 TYPE PER SITE (PROPOSAL, left-hand traffic, every ramp mirrored; each one is CHECKED by numbers in its own step and may be replaced)
- R4 tap, one minor leg, about 90 deg (T01, T02, T04, T08, T09, T13, T14, T17, T21 = 9): compact TRUMPET (one loop, minor road over the 8-lane road) or HALF DIAMOND with two slips; the loop on the side with free land (ground check). T21: trumpet with the long down-ramp of 21.7.
- R6 tap (T03, T10, T15, T22 = 4 sites; T11 is in a merge group): TRUMPET with 3 + 3 minor lanes; T15 has TWO minor roads at one point (R4-9 and R6-9, both end on M8 at 84 / 85 deg): one 4-leg-style tap with the R4 joined to the R6 stem before the ramps. T03: finish inside the built R6-8 half interchange (21.60 note). T10 is 61 deg: realign or Y-ramp.
- Merge groups (T05 + T06 + T07, T11 + T12, T16 + T18, T19 + T20 = 4 sites): trumpet or half diamond with a C-D road, type chosen in the group's own step.
- Ring S (4-leg): options run in RT-S-a with numbers (directional T + trumpet pair, or turbine-like), not chosen now.
- Ring N / E / W: ground level, the designs of HANDOFF_RING_T_JUNCTIONS.md (CGT / CFI / MUT), unchanged.
- N030, N074: grade-separated crossing with the structure of 21.8, ramps only where the class pair needs a connection (decided in the step).
- Conditional five: the ground-level signalised T of 21.48 is the base design; the interchange is designed only if the demand step fails them.
- 104 at-grade nodes: the 21.48 templates (priority T, T + pocket, roundabout 30 - 45 m or 50 - 70 m) built as a mesh in BATCHES of about 10 nodes per session, after the 20 interchanges.

### 24.5 ORDER AND MICRO-STEPS (one junction per session, STOP after each, the user says OK; the J1 / ring T pattern)
J-0 (this section) = count + rules, waiting for the user's OK / corrections (24.6).
J-1 PILOT = T14 (R4-8 on M8, st 2090). Why it is the cleanest: single minor leg, 87 deg, ring grade -1.05 %, levels equal 33.58 / 33.60, no merge partner, no arterial node within 400 m, 885 m from RT-S and 1485 m from T13. It proves the whole chain for the other single R4 taps.
Then (proposal): T22 (resume 21.60), T13, T17 (with N028), T14-like R4 taps, R6 taps T10 / T15 / T03, the 4 merge groups, ring S, N030 / N074 (with 21.8), ring N / E / W by their own handoff, the 5 conditional ones after the demand step, then the 104 at-grade batches.
Each junction runs these micro-steps (headless first, then the game, like J1-g1..g4):
 Jx-a SITE + DESIGN SHEET (numbers, picture, PASS / FAIL): ground (cut / fill, water, rail, buildings), vertical (minor over the main road: clearance 5.5 + deck, ramp grades, K, sight), type options with the numbers, decide the type.
 Jx-b GEOMETRY DATA: pure math in the arm frame, ramp centrelines (straight - spiral - arc - spiral - straight, R3), lane terminals (taper / decel / accel / weave from Standards.ts), checks with src/interchange/Validate.ts, R1 no-crossing check, 70 - 110 deg.
 Jx-c TERRAIN + STRUCTURES: pad / embankment / cut, bridge or retaining wall, drainage, join with the existing M8 / arm / minor road profile (same level AND grade at the join).
 Jx-d MESH + MARKING + GAME LAYER: ramps, gore, nose, kerb, barrier, arrows, stop lines, signs, lamps; config switch default OFF until the user has looked.
 Jx-e TRAFFIC GRAPH / AI: lanes joined (every movement reachable, no jump), remove the tLink stand-in for the arm if any, signal where the class wants it, check script.
Every step: R1 / R2 / R8 / R10 lines printed, tsc / npm run build / mission-test / test:tower run where node_modules exist, otherwise NOT RUN said plainly, this file updated (no new md), zip of only the changed files named by version.
